## Exhibit 99.1

Notice to LSE Total Voting Rights and Issued Capital 1 September 2026 In accordance with the Financial Conduct Authority’s (‘FCA’) Disclosure Guidance and Transparency Rule 5.6.1R, Rio Tinto plc notifies the market that as of 31 August 2026: 1. Rio Tinto plc’s issued share capital comprised 1,256,050,913 ordinary shares of 10p each, each with one vote. 2. 897,389 ordinary shares of 10p each are held in treasury. These shares are not taken into consideration in relation to the payment of dividends and voting at shareholder meetings. Accordingly, the total number of voting rights in Rio Tinto plc is 1,255,153,524. This figure may be used by shareholders (and others with notification obligations) as the denominator for the calculation by which they will determine if they are required to notify their interest in, or a change to their interest in, Rio Tinto plc under the FCA’s Disclosure Guidance and Transparency Rules. Note: As at the date of this announcement: (a) Rio Tinto plc has also issued one Special Voting Share of 10p and one DLC Dividend Share of 10p in connection with its dual listed companies (‘DLC’) merger with Rio Tinto Limited which was designed to place the shareholders of both companies in substantially the same position as if they held shares in a single enterprise owning all of the assets of both companies; (b) the Special Voting Share facilitates joint voting by shareholders of Rio Tinto plc and Rio Tinto Limited on joint electorate resolutions; and (c) there are 371,821,214 publicly held Rio Tinto Limited shares in issue which do not form part of the share capital of Rio Tinto plc. ## Exhibit 99.2

Notice to LSE Issue of Shares 1 September 2026 In accordance with PRM 1.6.4R, Rio Tinto plc (the "Company") (LEI:213800YOEO5OQ72G2R82) confirms that between 1 August 2026 and 31 August 2026, it has issued and allotted 209 ordinary shares of 10 pence each (ISIN GB0007188757) ("Shares") to satisfy awards under the Rio Tinto plc Global Employee Share Plan. The Shares were admitted to trading on the London Stock Exchange's Main Market under the Company’s existing block admission arrangements and rank equally with the Company’s existing ordinary shares. Following this issuance of Shares, the Company confirms that as at 31 August 2026, the Company's issued share capital comprised 1,256,050,913 Shares, each with one vote. 897,389 Shares are held in treasury. Accordingly, the total number of voting rights in the Company is 1,255,153,524. EXHIBIT 99.2

## Exhibit 99.3

EXHIBIT 99.3 Media Release Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation and Rio Tinto sign Interim Modernised Agreement 08 September 2026 KARRATHA, Australia--(BUSINESS WIRE)-- Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation and Rio Tinto have signed an Interim Modernised Agreement, strengthening their partnership and delivering long-term benefits for the Ngarlawangga People. This press release features multimedia. Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation Directors and Committee Members with Rio Tinto Iron Ore Traditional Owner Partnerships team at the signing on Country

From left to right: Caitlin Westgarth, Vice President HSEC Rio Tinto Iron Ore, Rishi Verma, General Manager West Angelas Rio Tinto Iron Ore, Damien Hubert and Albert Pianta, Directors Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation, Laura Thomas, General Manager Traditional Owner Partnerships Rio Tinto Iron Ore The interim agreement reflects a jointly designed co-management approach, underpinning how the two parties work together on Ngarlawangga Country within the agreement area, including earlier and ongoing engagement throughout the mine lifecycle. Using agreed processes, Ngarlawangga perspectives will shape decisions, giving all parties more certainty of how cultural heritage and Country will be managed and protected. The agreement also provides additional long-term financial support for the Ngarlawangga People to achieve their social, cultural and economic aspirations, with a strong focus on culture and community development. Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation said: “Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation is pleased to sign this agreement in partnership with Rio Tinto. This agreement highlights the strong partnership we have built over the years, and a relationship built on respect. We look forward to the next phase of implementing the agreement, and many more years of working together. “Our people have cared for Ngarlawangga Country for thousands of years. Through this agreement, Ngarlawangga people will continue to protect our cultural heritage and values, and carry out our responsibilities in looking after Country, as we work in partnership with Rio Tinto

through our jointly designed co-management approach, ensuring Ngarlawangga people continue to have a voice in how our cultural heritage is managed and maintained.” Rio Tinto Iron Ore Chief Executive Matthew Holcz said: “We thank the Ngarlawangga People for the trust and openness they have shown throughout this process. “This agreement is an important step in how we will work together to manage and protect Ngarlawangga heritage and Country. We will continue to listen, learn and work together to build a strong partnership that reflects Ngarlawangga aspirations and supports responsible operations on Country. “The Interim Modernised Agreement lays the foundation for a full modernised agreement. Ngarlawangga Aboriginal Corporation and Rio Tinto will continue working together to finalise that agreement.” The interim agreement builds on the 2011 Participation Agreement between the Ngarlawangga People and Rio Tinto. Rio Tinto West Angelas operations are located on the lands of the Yinhawangka and Ngarlawangga Peoples. ## Exhibit 99.4

EXHIBIT 99.4 Media Release Nyangumarta Warrarn Aboriginal Corporation and Rio Tinto sign milestone agreement for Winu Project 10 September 2026 PERTH, Australia--(BUSINESS WIRE)-- The Nyangumarta Warrarn Aboriginal Corporation (NWAC) and Rio Tinto have signed a Project Agreement for the proposed Winu copper-gold mine on Nyangumarta Country, marking a significant milestone in their long-term partnership and the development of the project. This press release features multimedia. View the full release here: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260909798343/en/ Nyangumarta Warrarn Aboriginal Corporation and community members gathered alongside representatives from the Yamatji Marlpa Aboriginal Corporation, Rio Tinto, and Sumitomo Metal Mining.

Building on the Project Planning Agreement signed in 2023, the newly co-designed agreement sets out how Nyangumarta People and Rio Tinto will work together, and how Nyangumarta knowledge, priorities and perspectives will inform the ongoing planning and implementation of activities on Country. This includes how potential impacts of the mine on the environment and Aboriginal cultural heritage will be avoided and minimised throughout project planning and future development. Through the agreement, Nyangumarta People have given their consent for the proposed Winu mine and associated infrastructure on Nyangumarta Country. The project remains subject to regulatory and other required approvals, and a final investment decision. Winu is anticipated to be the first mine developed on Nyangumarta Country, creating new pathways to training, employment and business participation. Nyangumarta and Rio Tinto will continue to explore economic opportunities that support community aspirations and respect Nyangumarta People’s strong connection to Country and culture. Winu is Rio Tinto's most advanced near-term greenfield copper project and an important part of the company's strategy to grow its copper portfolio. The project, located in Western Australia’s Great Sandy Desert, approximately 300 kilometres south of Broome, is a joint venture partnership with Sumitomo Metal Mining Co (SMM). Nyangumarta Elder Margaret (Nyaparu) Rose said the decision had been a long time in the making and carefully considered. “We’ve spent more than five years at the table with Nyangumarta People and Rio Tinto to make sure every voice is heard and everyone has all the information they need to make an informed decision. “Making sure our Country and culture are looked after has been at the heart of every conversation we’ve had about Winu. “The outcome of yesterday’s signing means we will have the means and resources to safeguard Country and provide opportunities for our people, both now and in the future.” Nyangumarta Elder Charlie Wright said the signing was a major milestone, but work will continue in making sure Nyangumarta People continue to have a strong voice in the project. “This agreement gives Nyangumarta People a seat at the table for the life of the project to ensure our culture, heritage, and Country is respected. “Through the opportunities with the project, we want to see our young people get jobs and training, our businesses get work, and our community sharing in the benefits from development on our Country. “We've worked hard to make sure our people were heard, and now the focus is on making sure these promises become a reality.”

Rio Tinto Copper Chief Executive Katie Jackson said: “The signing of this Agreement is an important step forward in advancing Winu towards first copper by 2030. “We sincerely thank Nyangumarta People for their partnership from the earliest days of Winu, and for the time, knowledge and perspectives they have shared to help shape the project and build the foundations of our long-term relationship. “This Agreement formalises how we will continue working together. It is not the conclusion of that work, and for Rio Tinto it reflects our ongoing responsibility to listen, to act with respect and to honour the commitments we have made." Under the joint venture arrangement, Rio Tinto will develop and operate Winu. Rio Tinto holds a 70 per cent interest and SMM holds a 30 per cent interest. View source version on businesswire.com: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260909798343/en/

603 Page 7 of 7 DIRECTIONS (1) If there are a number of substantial holders with similar or related relevant interests (eg. a corporation and its related corporations, or the manager and trustee of an equity trust), the names could be included in an annexure to the form. If the relevant interests of a group of persons are essentially similar, they may be referred to throughout the form as a specifically named group if the membership of each group, with the names and addresses of members is clearly set out in paragraph 7 of the form. (2) S ee the definition of "associate" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (3) S ee the definition of "relevant interest" in sections 608 and 671B(7) of the Corporations Act 2001. (4) T he voting shares of a company constitute one class unless divided into separate classes. (5) The total number of votes attached to all the voting shares in the company or voting interests in the scheme (if any) that the person or an associate has a relevant interest in. (6) T he person's votes divided by the total votes in the body corporate or scheme multiplied by 100. (7) Include details of: (a) any relevant agreement or other circumstances by which the relevant interest was acquired. If subsection 671B(4) applies, a copy of any document setting out the terms of any relevant agreement, and a statement by the person giving full and accurate details of any contract, scheme or arrangement, must accompany this form, together with a written statement certifying this contract, scheme or arrangement; and (b) any qualification of the power of a person to exercise, control the exercise of, or influence the exercise of, the voting powers or disposal of the securities to which the relevant interest relates (indicating clearly the particular securities to which the qualification applies). See the definition of "relevant agreement" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (8) If the substantial holder is unable to determine the identity of the person ( eg. if the relevant interest arises because of an option) write "unknown". (9) Details of the consideration must include any and all benefits, money and other, that any person from whom a relevant interest was acquired has, or may, become entitled to receive in relation to that acquisition. Details must be included even if the benefit is conditional on the happening or not of a contingency. Details must be included of any benefit paid on behalf of the substantial holder or its associate in relation to the acquisitions, even if they are not paid directly to the person from whom the relevant interest was acquired.

Appendix: Prescribed information pursuant to securities lending arrangement disclosed under the substantial shareholding notice filed with ASX. Date: 15-Sep-2026 Company’s name: RIO TINTO LTD ISIN: AU000000RIO1 Date of change of relevant interests: 11-Sep-2026 Schedule Type of agreement Global Master Securities Lending Agreement ("GMSLA") Parties to agreement JPMorgan Chase Bank, N.A. (acting as agent) (“lender”) and BNP Paribas Financial Markets (Borrower) Transfer date Settlement Date 15-Jun-2026 24-Jun-2026 26-Jun-2026 29-Jun-2026 30-Jun-2026 01-Jul-2026 03-Jul-2026 07-Jul-2026 08-Jul-2026 09-Jul-2026 29-Jul-2026 18-Aug-2026 24-Aug-2026 25-Aug-2026 26-Aug-2026 28-Aug-2026 31-Aug-2026 04-Sep-2026 08-Sep-2026 09-Sep-2026 11-Sep-2026 Holder of voting rights Borrower Are there any restriction on voting rights Yes If yes, detail The borrower undertakes to use its best endeavors to arrange for the voting rights to be exercised in accordance with the instructions of the lender, provided that the lender uses its best endeavors to notify the borrower of its instructions in writing no later than 7 business days prior to the date upon which such votes are exercisable or as otherwise agreed between the parties. This undertaking is set out in clause 6.6 of the standard form GMSLA. Scheduled return date (if any) None Does the borrower have the right to return early? Yes If yes, detail Borrower has right to return all and any equivalent securities early at any time in accordance with the lender's instructions. Does the lender have the right to recall early? Yes If yes, detail Lender has right to recall all or any equivalent securities at any time by giving notice on any business day of not less than the standard settlement time for such equivalent securities on the exchange or in the clearing organisation through which the relevant borrowed securities were originally delivered (and where there is a difference between the settlement time for sales and purchases on the relevant exchange or clearing organisation, the standard settlement time shall be the shorter of the two times). The borrower must return the securities not later than the expiry of such notice in accordance with the lender's instructions.

Will the securities be returned on settlement? Yes If yes, detail any exceptions No exceptions Statement If requested by the company to whom the prescribed form must be given, or if requested by ASIC, a copy of the agreement will be given to that company or ASIC.

(2) See the definition of "relevant interest" in sections 608 and 671B(7) of the Corporations Act 2001. (3) See the definition of "associate" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (4) Include details of: (a) any relevant agreement or other circumstances because of which the change in relevant interest occurred. If subsection 671B(4) applies, a copy of any document setting out the terms of any relevant agreement, and a statement by the person giving full and accurate details of any contract, scheme or arrangement, must accompany this form, together with a written statement certifying this contract, scheme or arrangement; and (b) any qualification of the power of a person to exercise, control the exercise of, or influence the exercise of, the voting powers or disposal of the securities to which the relevant interest relates (indicating clearly the particular securities to which the qualification applies). See the definition of "relevant agreement" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (5) Details of the consideration must include any and all benefits, money and other, that any person from whom a relevant interest was acquired has, or may, become entitled to receive in relation to that acquisition. Details must be included even if the benefit is conditional n the happening or not of a contingency. Details must be included of any benefit paid on behalf of the substantial holder or its associate in relation to the acquisitions, even if they are not paid directly to the person from whom the relevant interest was acquired. (6) T he voting shares of a company constitute one class unless divided into separate classes. (7) Give details, if appropriate, of the present association and any change in that association since the last substantial holding notice.

Date: 22-Sep-2026 Company’s name: RIO TINTO LTD ISIN: AU000000RIO1 Date of change of relevant interests: 18-Sep-2026 Schedule Type of agreement Global Master Securities Lending Agreement ("GMSLA") Parties to agreement JPMorgan Chase Bank, N.A. (acting as agent) (“lender”) and BNP Paribas Financial Markets (Borrower) Transfer date Settlement Date 15-Jun-2026 24-Jun-2026 26-Jun-2026 29-Jun-2026 30-Jun-2026 01-Jul-2026 03-Jul-2026 07-Jul-2026 08-Jul-2026 09-Jul-2026 29-Jul-2026 18-Aug-2026 24-Aug-2026 25-Aug-2026 26-Aug-2026 28-Aug-2026 31-Aug-2026 04-Sep-2026 08-Sep-2026 09-Sep-2026 11-Sep-2026 18-Sep-2026 21-Sep-2026 Holder of voting rights Borrower Are there any restriction on voting rights Yes If yes, detail The borrower undertakes to use its best endeavors to arrange for the voting rights to be exercised in accordance with the instructions of the lender, provided that the lender uses its best endeavors to notify the borrower of its instructions in writing no later than 7 business days prior to the date upon which such votes are exercisable or as otherwise agreed between the parties. This undertaking is set out in clause 6.6 of the standard form GMSLA. Scheduled return date (if any) None Does the borrower have the right to return early? Yes If yes, detail Borrower has right to return all and any equivalent securities early at any time in accordance with the lender's instructions. Does the lender have the right to recall early? Yes

If yes, detail Lender has right to recall all or any equivalent securities at any time by giving notice on any business day of not less than the standard settlement time for such equivalent securities on the exchange or in the clearing organisation through which the relevant borrowed securities were originally delivered (and where there is a difference between the settlement time for sales and purchases on the relevant exchange or clearing organisation, the standard settlement time shall be the shorter of the two times). The borrower must return the securities not later than the expiry of such notice in accordance with the lender's instructions. Will the securities be returned on settlement? Yes If yes, detail any exceptions No exceptions Statement If requested by the company to whom the prescribed form must be given, or if requested by ASIC, a copy of the agreement will be given to that company or ASIC.

## Exhibit 99.13

EXHIBIT 99.13 Media Release Rio Tinto and RevoCast partner to expand low-carbon aluminium billet supply in North America 28 September 2026 VANCOUVER, Canada – Rio Tinto and RevoCast Aluminum Billets Ltd. have entered into a long-term agreement to expand the supply of low-carbon aluminium billet to extrusion customers across North America. Under the agreement, Rio Tinto will exclusively market billet produced at RevoCast’s newly commissioned casting facility in Langley, British Columbia. The offering will combine Rio Tinto’s low-carbon primary aluminium, produced using hydropower at its Kitimat operations, with recycled aluminium processed by RevoCast. This combination will give manufacturers access to high-quality, lower-carbon billet produced closer to their operations, while strengthening regional supply chains and supporting greater circularity in British Columbia’s construction sector. Matt Schicke, Vice President, Aluminium Sales and Marketing at Rio Tinto, said: “By combining Rio Tinto’s low-carbon aluminium production in British Columbia with RevoCast’s recycling and casting capabilities, we can provide North American manufacturers with a reliable supply of high-quality billet produced closer to their operations. This partnership strengthens our ability to help customers increase the recycled and low-carbon content of their products and build more resilient value chains.” Thomas Martini, President and Chief Executive Officer of RevoCast, said: “Our partnership with Rio Tinto marks an important milestone for RevoCast, bringing together our advanced remelting capabilities with Rio Tinto’s global expertise, market knowledge and reach. Together, we are expanding access to reliable, high-quality, low-carbon billet with recycled content for extrusion customers across North America, while helping strengthen the region’s aluminium supply chain.” Commissioned in August 2026, RevoCast’s Langley facility has annual production capacity of more than 80,000 tonnes of 6xxx-series aluminium billet for extrusion applications in construction, transportation, industrial products and consumer goods. The facility uses recycled aluminium and advanced remelting technology designed to maximize energy efficiency and reduce emissions.

Notice to ASX/LSE Shareholdings of persons discharging managerial responsibility (PDMR) / Key Management Personnel (KMP) 29 September 2026 As part of its dual listed company structure, Rio Tinto notifies dealings in Rio Tinto plc and Rio Tinto Limited securities by PDMRs / KMPs to both the Australian Securities Exchange (ASX) and the London Stock Exchange (LSE). Share Plan Account (SPA) Rio Tinto plc ordinary shares of 10p each and Rio Tinto Limited ordinary shares held in a SPA are eligible for a dividend reinvestment whereby cash dividends are used to buy Rio Tinto shares in the market. On 24 September 2026 the following PDMR/KMPs acquired shares in Rio Tinto plc by way of reinvestment of dividends received on shares held in the SPA. Rio Tinto plc shares are purchased out of monthly deductions from salary, and participants are allocated an equivalent number of shares free of charge (matching shares). Qualifying UK employees are also awarded Free Shares once a year. Rio Tinto plc shares held in the UKSP are subject to dividend reinvestment whereby cash dividends are used to buy Rio Tinto plc shares in the market.
